It focuses on classifying COVID-19 and lung cancer using chest X-ray images.  The approach utilizes high-capacity teacher models (VGG19, Visformer-S, AutoFormer-V2-T) to train a lightweight student model derived from the OFA-595 supernet. The training uses a hybrid loss function combining ground truth labels and teacher-generated soft targets. Explainability is achieved through Score-CAM visualizations, offering insights into the model's decision-making process. The authors validate their approach on COVID-QU-Ex and LCS25000 datasets, showing that the distilled student model maintains performance with significantly reduced computational requirements and increased interpretability.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Strengths:**

    * **Addresses a relevant problem:**  The paper tackles a crucial challenge in medical AI: deploying complex models in resource-constrained clinical settings. The combination of knowledge distillation (for efficiency) and explainability (for trust) is a practical direction.
    * **Comprehensive Experimental Setup:** The paper explores different teacher model architectures, a hybrid loss function, and evaluates on two separate medical imaging datasets (CXR and histopathology), demonstrating the robustness of their proposed KD framework.
    * **Quantifiable Results:** The paper presents clear and quantitative results, showing the performance of both teacher and student models in terms of AUC, Accuracy, and F1-score, and includes visualization results using ScoreCAM to better understand the decisions the model is making.
    * **Addresses important evaluation aspects:** The authors addressed the important point of over-fitting by utilizing regularization and dropout techniques to prevent model over-fitting which can result in improved generalizability of the model.
    * **Multi-objective approach:** The integration of knowledge distillation with interpretable AI (XAI) is a strong point, addressing both efficiency and trust issues.

* **Weaknesses:**

    * **Limited Novelty:**  The components of the framework (knowledge distillation, Score-CAM, specific teacher/student architectures) are not individually novel. The primary novelty lies in their specific *combination* and application to the medical imaging domain. However, many papers have combined KD and XAI before, so the novelty is somewhat limited.
    * **Incremental Advance:** The specific gains achieved by the proposed framework over standard knowledge distillation methods are not strongly emphasized. It would be more convincing to see direct comparisons with other KD techniques specifically designed for medical imaging, demonstrating the *added value* of the explainability component.
    * **Scope of Explainability:** Score-CAM provides a visualization of where the model is looking, but it doesn't necessarily explain *why* the model made a specific decision.  The interpretability aspect could be enhanced by incorporating more sophisticated XAI methods or by conducting human-in-the-loop studies to assess the clinical utility of the explanations.
    * **Limited Discussion of Clinical Relevance:** While the paper aims for practical clinical deployment, there is little discussion of the specific clinical contexts where this framework would be most useful. Who are the intended users of the XAI output, and how would the explanations impact their decision-making?
    * **Dataset limitations:** The reliance on two specific datasets (COVID-QU-Ex and LCS25000) limits the generalizability of the findings. While the authors acknowledge this, further validation on more diverse datasets and disease types is crucial.

* **Significance:**

   The paper has the potential to contribute to the development of more accessible and trustworthy medical AI systems. By demonstrating the feasibility of distilling knowledge into lightweight models while maintaining interpretability, it can pave the way for deploying these systems in resource-limited environments where access to expert clinicians is limited.

**Justification for Score:**

I am assigning a score of **6/10**.

*   The paper tackles a relevant and important problem with a practical approach. The experimental results are clearly presented and demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed framework.
*   However, the individual components lack significant novelty, and the incremental gains compared to standard KD methods are not rigorously established. The scope of the explainability component is also limited. Further, the clinical relevance of the explainability aspect could be bolstered. The work lacks substantial new concepts, methods or insights.

The paper presents a reasonable step forward, but its novelty is limited and significance is incremental. It needs stronger validation across more diverse datasets and more robust analysis of its added value within a clinical workflow to gain greater impact.

- **Classification**: eess.IV
- **Score**: 0/10

### Conflict-Aware Soft Prompting for Retrieval-Augmented Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15253v1)
- **Authors**: Eunseong Choi, June Park, Hyeri Lee, Jongwuk Lee
- **Abstract**: Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) enhances the capabilities of large language models (LLMs) by incorporating external knowledge into their input prompts. However, when the retrieved context contradicts the LLM's parametric knowledge, it often fails to resolve the conflict between incorrect external context and correct parametric knowledge, known as context-memory conflict. To tackle this problem, we introduce Conflict-Aware REtrieval-Augmented Generation (CARE), consisting of a context assessor and a base LLM. The context assessor encodes compact memory token embeddings from raw context tokens. Through grounded/adversarial soft prompting, the context assessor is trained to discern unreliable context and capture a guidance signal that directs reasoning toward the more reliable knowledge source. Extensive experiments show that CARE effectively mitigates context-memory conflicts, leading to an average performance gain of 5.0\% on QA and fact-checking benchmarks, establishing a promising direction for trustworthy and adaptive RAG systems.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Conflict-Aware Retrieval-Augmented Generation (CARE), a novel approach to mitigate the context-memory conflict in Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) systems. This conflict arises when the retrieved external context contradicts the internal parametric knowledge of the Large Language Model (LLM), leading to performance degradation. CARE addresses this by incorporating a context assessor, which encodes external context into compact memory embeddings. Through grounded/adversarial soft prompting, the context assessor learns to discern unreliable context and guide the LLM towards the more reliable knowledge source. The paper presents experimental results demonstrating that CARE effectively mitigates context-memory conflicts, leading to improved performance on QA and fact-checking benchmarks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of introducing a context assessor trained with a conflict-aware approach to guide the LLM in RAG systems is relatively novel.  While prior work addresses context-memory conflict, the combination of a soft-prompted assessor trained via *both* grounded *and* adversarial training is a differentiating factor. The paper effectively repurposes soft prompting not just for compression but for reflecting reliability, a less explored avenue.

*   **Significance:** The context-memory conflict is a significant problem in RAG systems, especially as they are deployed in real-world scenarios where retrieved context can be noisy or inaccurate. By addressing this issue, CARE has the potential to improve the trustworthiness and reliability of RAG systems, making them more suitable for applications where accuracy is critical. The paper demonstrates substantial performance gains across multiple tasks and datasets.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Well-defined problem:** The paper clearly articulates the context-memory conflict and its impact on RAG performance.
    *   **Clear methodology:**  The CARE architecture and training procedure are well-explained, with sufficient details for reproducibility.
    *   **Comprehensive experiments:** The paper includes a wide range of experiments across different tasks, datasets, and LLMs, demonstrating the robustness of the proposed approach.
    *   **Ablation studies:** The ablation studies effectively demonstrate the contribution of each component of CARE.
    *   **Qualitative analysis:** The visualization of context embeddings provides insights into how CARE learns to distinguish between positive and negative contexts.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited scope:** The paper primarily focuses on the top-1 retrieved passage setting, minimizing other potential issues like inter-context contradictions when multiple passages are retrieved.  While the authors provide a preliminary result with the top-3 retrieved passages, further investigation is needed.
    *   **Reliance on closed-book accuracy for training:** The training process relies on closed-book accuracy as a signal for conflict. While reasonable, this proxy might not be perfectly accurate, potentially leading to suboptimal training. More robust methods for detecting knowledge conflicts could be explored.
    *   **Potential for increased complexity:** The introduction of a context assessor adds complexity to the RAG pipeline.  Although the paper shows efficiency analyses, the added overhead in terms of training and deployment costs should be considered.
    *   **Hyperparameter tuning details:**  While most hyperparameters are given, more details on the method to find them could be provided.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to influence future research on RAG systems, particularly in the area of conflict resolution. The idea of using a trained assessor to guide the LLM could be extended to other scenarios, such as multi-document summarization or dialogue generation.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:** The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of RAG by addressing the context-memory conflict with a well-designed and thoroughly evaluated approach.  The soft-prompted context assessor trained with grounded and adversarial learning is a clever idea that demonstrates promising results. While the paper has some limitations, such as its focus on top-1 retrieval and reliance on closed-book accuracy for training, its strengths outweigh its weaknesses. CARE has the potential to be a valuable tool for improving the trustworthiness and reliability of RAG systems, and is likely to inspire further research in this area.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Deep Think with Confidence
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15260v1)
- **Authors**: Yichao Fu, Xuewei Wang, Yuandong Tian, Jiawei Zhao
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have shown great potential in reasoning tasks through test-time scaling methods like self-consistency with majority voting. However, this approach often leads to diminishing returns in accuracy and high computational overhead. To address these challenges, we introduce Deep Think with Confidence (DeepConf), a simple yet powerful method that enhances both reasoning efficiency and performance at test time. DeepConf leverages model-internal confidence signals to dynamically filter out low-quality reasoning traces during or after generation. It requires no additional model training or hyperparameter tuning and can be seamlessly integrated into existing serving frameworks. We evaluate DeepConf across a variety of reasoning tasks and the latest open-source models, including Qwen 3 and GPT-OSS series. Notably, on challenging benchmarks such as AIME 2025, DeepConf@512 achieves up to 99.9% accuracy and reduces generated tokens by up to 84.7% compared to full parallel thinking.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Deep Think with Confidence":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DeepConf, a test-time method designed to enhance the reasoning efficiency and performance of Large Language Models (LLMs). DeepConf leverages model-internal confidence signals to dynamically filter out low-quality reasoning traces during or after generation, thereby reducing computational overhead. The method requires no additional model training or hyperparameter tuning and can be integrated into existing serving frameworks. The authors evaluate DeepConf across a variety of reasoning tasks and LLMs, demonstrating significant reductions in token generation and improvements in accuracy, especially on challenging benchmarks like AIME 2025. The core idea is to use localized confidence measures rather than global trace confidence, which allows for earlier stopping and reduced computational cost. DeepConf can be used in both offline, by re-evaluating already generated traces to boost performance; and online to stop generations before completion.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of using model confidence for filtering reasoning traces isn't entirely new.  Previous works have explored global confidence measures for this purpose. However, DeepConf's **primary novelty lies in its focus on localized (group level) confidence and the integration of this localized measure into an *online* setting enabling early stopping and adapting the amount of generated traces.**  Previous methods relied on analyzing entire traces, limiting their online applicability.  The proposed bottom-10% and tail-confidence metrics are also interesting variations that better capture potentially problematic zones in generation.  Therefore the incremental improvements and the proposed *online* setting are not particularly groundbreaking, but valuable in improving efficiency.

*   **Significance:** Reducing the computational cost of inference with LLMs, particularly for complex reasoning tasks, is a highly significant problem.  Methods that can achieve accuracy improvements or maintenance with significantly fewer tokens are directly relevant to practitioners deploying these models.  The paper's focus on test-time methods is also practically relevant, as it avoids the need for costly retraining or fine-tuning.  If the claims about seamless integration into serving frameworks are accurate, it adds considerable practical value.
The paper offers compelling experimental results, including a saturation of performance on a challenging benchmark (AIME 2025). This demonstrates the method's potential in practical scenarios.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Practical Focus:** The paper emphasizes a simple, easy-to-implement method that can be deployed with existing LLMs and serving frameworks.
    *   **Significant Efficiency Gains:**  The experimental results demonstrate substantial reductions in token generation.
    *   **Good Empirical Validation:**  Evaluated across multiple datasets and models, with careful averaging to ensure robustness.
    *   **Clear Online Setting:** Enabling practical applicability.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Incremental Novelty:** As mentioned, the core concept builds upon existing work. The added value comes from the detailed metrics and the *online* early stopping adaptation.
    *   **Generality of Threshold:**  The paper relies on setting a confidence threshold via an offline warmup phase. There are potential concerns about the robustness and transferability of this threshold across different problem instances and domains.  More discussion or experimentation on the impact of threshold selection would be valuable. Are those well calibrated?
    *   **Limited Analysis of Failure Cases:**  While the paper reports overall accuracy improvements, it provides less insight into *when* and *why* DeepConf fails. Are there specific types of reasoning problems where early stopping degrades performance significantly? How does it affect different types of errors?

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to influence the development of more efficient inference techniques for LLMs. The focus on localized confidence and early stopping provides a promising direction for future research. Given the growing importance of LLM deployment, this work could contribute to wider adoption by reducing computational costs.

*   **Rigorous Rationale:** While the paper provides good results and improves upon the existing baseline, the novelty lies in the online evaluation, instead of creating a novel underlying methodology.

**Score: 7**

*Rationale:* DeepConf presents valuable, incremental improvements to LLM inference, offering a practical test-time method for enhancing reasoning efficiency. Its strengths lie in the simplicity of implementation, solid experimental results, and relevance to real-world deployment. The online capabilities, which enable real-time adaptation, are particularly noteworthy.
However, the limitations in regards to threshold calibration, and limited failure analysis prevent a higher score. The incremental contribution compared to existing approaches reduces the overall significance of the work. Overall, the work is a useful contribution that should be pursued in the community, but does not merit a score above a seven, due to the limited novel evaluation setting.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### M-$LLM^3$REC: A Motivation-Aware User-Item Interaction Framework for Enhancing Recommendation Accuracy with LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15262v1)
- **Authors**: Lining Chen, Qingwen Zeng, Huaming Chen
- **Abstract**: Recommendation systems have been essential for both user experience and platform efficiency by alleviating information overload and supporting decision-making. Traditional methods, i.e., content-based filtering, collaborative filtering, and deep learning, have achieved impressive results in recommendation systems. However, the cold-start and sparse-data scenarios are still challenging to deal with. Existing solutions either generate pseudo-interaction sequence, which often introduces redundant or noisy signals, or rely heavily on semantic similarity, overlooking dynamic shifts in user motivation. To address these limitations, this paper proposes a novel recommendation framework, termed M-$LLM^3$REC, which leverages large language models for deep motivational signal extraction from limited user interactions. M-$LLM^3$REC comprises three integrated modules: the Motivation-Oriented Profile Extractor (MOPE), Motivation-Oriented Trait Encoder (MOTE), and Motivational Alignment Recommender (MAR). By emphasizing motivation-driven semantic modeling, M-$LLM^3$REC demonstrates robust, personalized, and generalizable recommendations, particularly boosting performance in cold-start situations in comparison with the state-of-the-art frameworks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "M-LLM³REC: A Motivation-Aware User-Item Interaction Framework for Enhancing Recommendation Accuracy with LLMs" proposes a new recommendation framework designed to address the cold-start and sparse data problems. The core idea is to leverage large language models (LLMs) to extract deep motivational signals from limited user interactions, rather than relying solely on historical behavior or semantic similarity. The framework consists of three modules: a Motivation-Oriented Profile Extractor (MOPE), a Motivation-Oriented Trait Encoder (MOTE), and a Motivational Alignment Recommender (MAR). These modules work together to infer user motivations, abstract item traits, and align them for personalized recommendations. The authors claim that M-LLM³REC demonstrates improved accuracy, generalizability, and interpretability, particularly in cold-start scenarios, compared to state-of-the-art recommendation systems. The paper empirically validates the framework through extensive experiments on several benchmark datasets.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a genuinely novel approach by directly modeling user motivations as a core component of a recommendation system.  While using LLMs for recommendation is not new, the explicit focus on motivational extraction and alignment is a distinct contribution. The framework components (MOPE, MOTE, MAR) and their integration present a well-structured and logically sound design. The departure from traditional behavior-centric paradigms is a welcome shift. The concept of distilling item traits into motivational categories is also a solid and innovative idea.

*   **Significance:** Addressing the cold-start problem is of paramount importance in the recommendation domain. The paper's claimed improvements in accuracy and generalizability in sparse-data scenarios, if substantiated by independent replication, could have a significant impact on real-world applications. The modular design of the framework lends itself to future extensions and adaptations. The emphasis on interpretability, through motivation-to-trait alignment, is also significant, as it can help users understand why certain items are being recommended. The introduction of the reflective prompting technique is significant. The study also presents novel ablation studies and a hybrid model selection strategy.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The motivational modeling approach is theoretically well-motivated and offers a plausible alternative to traditional methods.
    *   The modular framework is well-defined and promotes transparency and extensibility.
    *   The paper provides a comprehensive empirical evaluation across multiple datasets and baselines, demonstrating the superiority of M-LLM³REC.
    *   The cost-performance analysis is a valuable addition, considering the computational demands of LLMs.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The reliance on LLMs introduces computational overhead. While the paper discusses cost reduction strategies, the overall computational cost may still be prohibitive for some applications.
    *   The choice of GPT models is a potential limitation, since other LLMs might yield different results. It would strengthen the study to include results across several different LLMs.
    *   While the paper presents results for user cold-start and item cold-start scenarios, it would be useful to show its efficacy and results on the tail-end long item scenario.
    *   While the results are encouraging, the improvements could be, at least partially, due to specific parameter choices/prompting. Future work could include automated prompt engineering strategies.

*   **Potential Influence:**

    *   The paper's focus on motivation-aware recommendation could inspire new research directions in the field, moving beyond purely behavioral or semantic approaches.
    *   The M-LLM³REC framework could serve as a blueprint for building more robust and interpretable recommendation systems.
    *   The insights gained from this work could be applied to other areas of information retrieval and personalized search.

*   **Overall Assessment:**

    The paper presents a compelling and innovative approach to recommendation, leveraging the power of LLMs to model user motivations. The experimental results are promising and suggest that M-LLM³REC has the potential to outperform existing methods, particularly in challenging cold-start scenarios. The weaknesses related to computational cost and prompting robustness should be carefully addressed in future research. The model's interpretability and strong potential impact contribute to the significance of this work.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.IR
- **Score**: 7/10

### TComQA: Extracting Temporal Commonsense from Text
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15274v1)
- **Authors**: Lekshmi R Nair, Arun Sankar, Koninika Pal
- **Abstract**: Understanding events necessitates grasping their temporal context, which is often not explicitly stated in natural language. For example, it is not a trivial task for a machine to infer that a museum tour may last for a few hours, but can not take months. Recent studies indicate that even advanced large language models (LLMs) struggle in generating text that require reasoning with temporal commonsense due to its infrequent explicit mention in text. Therefore, automatically mining temporal commonsense for events enables the creation of robust language models. In this work, we investigate the capacity of LLMs to extract temporal commonsense from text and evaluate multiple experimental setups to assess their effectiveness. Here, we propose a temporal commonsense extraction pipeline that leverages LLMs to automatically mine temporal commonsense and use it to construct TComQA, a dataset derived from SAMSum and RealNews corpora. TComQA has been validated through crowdsourcing and achieves over 80\% precision in extracting temporal commonsense. The model trained with TComQA also outperforms an LLM fine-tuned on existing dataset of temporal question answering task.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces TComQA, a new dataset for temporal commonsense reasoning. The authors propose an automated pipeline that leverages large language models (LLMs) to extract temporal commonsense from text.  The pipeline consists of three key components: a question generation module (fine-tuned T5), a question validation module (based on lexical and semantic similarity), and an answer generation module (fine-tuned Llama2). The TComQA dataset is derived from the SAMSum and RealNews corpora and is validated through crowdsourcing. Experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed pipeline and the utility of TComQA in improving temporal question answering performance.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of automatically extracting temporal commonsense using LLMs is not entirely novel. Previous works have explored similar approaches, although perhaps not with this specific pipeline architecture. The key novelty lies in the specific implementation, the use of Llama2 for answer generation, and the introduction of the TComQA dataset. The use of both lexical and semantic validation techniques for question generation filtering is a good contribution.

*   **Significance:** The development of a high-quality temporal commonsense dataset is a valuable contribution to the field. Such datasets are crucial for training and evaluating language models' ability to reason about time and events.  The automated extraction pipeline helps address the scalability issues associated with manual annotation, allowing for the creation of larger and more diverse datasets.  The paper demonstrates that the TComQA dataset can improve the performance of a question-answering model, which further highlights its utility.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The proposed pipeline is well-defined and modular, making it easier to understand and adapt.
    *   The use of LLMs for both question generation and answer generation is a strong approach.
    *   The inclusion of both lexical and semantic validation metrics improves the quality of the generated questions.
    *   The crowd-sourced validation of the TComQA dataset provides evidence of its quality.
    *   The paper clearly explains the experimental setup and presents the results in a comprehensive manner.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the paper demonstrates improvement over existing datasets (MCTACO), it does not show that using TComQA leads to significantly better performance in downstream applications. Evaluating on a real-world task would provide more evidence. The evaluation is relatively limited in scope.
    *   The paper could benefit from a more in-depth analysis of the types of temporal commonsense that the pipeline is able to extract effectively, and the types that it struggles with.  Error analysis is needed to understand the limitations.
    *   The evaluation metrics could be improved. Semantic similarity is a coarse-grained measure, and may not fully capture the nuances of temporal reasoning.
    *   The discussion of related work could be more thorough, particularly in terms of comparing the proposed approach to other automated knowledge extraction techniques.
    *   The authors mention sharing the code and dataset, however, the provided "here" link doesn't actually include it (it has been "replaced" with placeholder, so the research cannot be fully reproduced by others without additional effort). This reduces the potential impact/replicability of the study.

**Justification for Score:**

While the paper has some strong points, specifically in creating a temporal dataset and utilizing LLMs in a modular pipeline, the lack of a compelling real-world task, limited analysis of errors and lack of code limit the overall impact. The novelty, while present, is incremental rather than groundbreaking. The significance is primarily in dataset creation, but it could be greatly improved by showing actual impact on real temporal question answering rather than a direct improvement on another simpler QA dataset.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### AmbiSQL: Interactive Ambiguity Detection and Resolution for Text-to-SQL
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15276v1)
- **Authors**: Zhongjun Ding, Yin Lin, Tianjing Zeng
- **Abstract**: Text-to-SQL systems translate natural language questions into SQL queries, providing substantial value for non-expert users. While large language models (LLMs) show promising results for this task, they remain error-prone. Query ambiguity has been recognized as a major obstacle for LLM-based Text-to-SQL systems, leading to misinterpretation of user intent and inaccurate SQL generation. We demonstrate AmbiSQL, an interactive system that automatically detects query ambiguities and guides users through intuitive multiple-choice questions to clarify their intent. Our approach introduces a fine-grained ambiguity taxonomy for identifying ambiguities that affect database element mapping and LLM reasoning, then incorporates user feedback to rewrite ambiguous questions. Evaluation on an ambiguous query dataset shows that AmbiSQL achieves 87.2% precision in ambiguity detection and improves SQL exact match accuracy by 50% when integrated with Text-to-SQL systems. Our demonstration showcases the significant performance gains and highlights the system's practical usability. Code repo and demonstration are available at: https://github.com/JustinzjDing/AmbiSQL.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the "AMBISQL: Interactive Ambiguity Detection and Resolution for Text-to-SQL" paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces AMBISQL, an interactive system designed to improve the accuracy of Text-to-SQL systems by addressing query ambiguity. It presents a fine-grained ambiguity taxonomy encompassing both database element mapping and LLM reasoning aspects. AMBISQL uses in-context learning to detect ambiguous phrases, classifies them, generates targeted clarification questions for users, and rewrites queries based on their responses.  The system integrates as a preprocessing module with existing Text-to-SQL systems and demonstrates significant improvements in SQL generation accuracy and user-friendliness. The authors evaluate AMBISQL on a set of ambiguous queries derived from the BIRD and TAG benchmarks, demonstrating high precision in ambiguity detection and substantial gains in SQL exact match accuracy.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in its comprehensive ambiguity taxonomy, particularly its inclusion of LLM reasoning ambiguities, a point often overlooked in previous Text-to-SQL ambiguity research. The interactive approach, while not entirely new, is well-executed and significantly improves accuracy compared to purely automated methods. The system also distinguishes itself by generating targeted clarification questions rather than generic ones or simply generating all possible interpretations. The integrated system and the analysis of its performance on existing benchmarks provide empirical evidence of its value.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a significant problem in Text-to-SQL: the ambiguity inherent in natural language. By providing a practical system that can detect and resolve ambiguity through user interaction, AMBISQL has the potential to improve the usability and reliability of Text-to-SQL systems. The empirical results are compelling, showing substantial gains in accuracy when AMBISQL is integrated with an existing Text-to-SQL system (XiYan-SQL). This confirms that the interactive ambiguity resolution can be very useful. The practical demonstration and publicly available code enhance its significance. The work could promote more user-centric development of Text-to-SQL systems.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Ambiguity Taxonomy:** The taxonomy is well-defined and covers both database-related and LLM-related ambiguities.
    *   **Interactive Approach:**  Engaging users in the ambiguity resolution process is crucial for aligning with user intent.
    *   **Targeted Clarification Questions:** Generates multiple-choice questions specifically tailored to the identified ambiguities.
    *   **Empirical Evaluation:**  Demonstrates significant accuracy improvements on ambiguous queries.
    *   **Practical System:**  Provides a user-friendly interface and publicly available code, increasing its real-world applicability.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **LLM Reliance:** While using LLMs for ambiguity detection is efficient, it's also a potential source of error and bias. The system's performance depends on the LLM's capabilities.
    *   **User Burden:** The interactive nature requires users to answer clarification questions. While necessary for accuracy, this might become tedious for complex or highly ambiguous queries. The paper should explore ways to minimize the number of required interactions. The paper also does not conduct a user study to examine the effect on user effort required to obtain the desired query.
    *   **Benchmark Limitations:** The evaluation is limited to ambiguous queries derived from BIRD and TAG. While these are standard benchmarks, they may not fully represent the diversity of ambiguities encountered in real-world applications.
    *   **Integration Cost and Latency:** The paper should more thoroughly address the performance overhead of the additional ambiguity detection and resolution stages when integrated with existing Text-to-SQL systems. The real-time response latency is important to user interaction and experience.
    *   **Scalability Issues:** The paper does not explicitly address the scalability of the system to very large databases or highly complex queries. Further analysis is needed to ensure that the system can be deployed in real-world scenarios.

*   **Potential Influence:**  The paper's approach could influence the development of more user-friendly and accurate Text-to-SQL systems. The ambiguity taxonomy and interactive resolution techniques could be adopted by other researchers and practitioners in the field. The work also underscores the importance of considering LLM reasoning ambiguities in Text-to-SQL systems.

*   **Rationale:** The paper offers a novel and practical approach to a persistent problem in Text-to-SQL. While it has some limitations (LLM dependence, user burden), the strengths outweigh the weaknesses. The clear contributions, compelling empirical results, and potential for real-world impact warrant a positive evaluation.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.DB
- **Score**: 7/10

### Adversarial Attacks against Neural Ranking Models via In-Context Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15283v1)
- **Authors**: Amin Bigdeli, Negar Arabzadeh, Ebrahim Bagheri, Charles L. A. Clarke
- **Abstract**: While neural ranking models (NRMs) have shown high effectiveness, they remain susceptible to adversarial manipulation. In this work, we introduce Few-Shot Adversarial Prompting (FSAP), a novel black-box attack framework that leverages the in-context learning capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs) to generate high-ranking adversarial documents. Unlike previous approaches that rely on token-level perturbations or manual rewriting of existing documents, FSAP formulates adversarial attacks entirely through few-shot prompting, requiring no gradient access or internal model instrumentation. By conditioning the LLM on a small support set of previously observed harmful examples, FSAP synthesizes grammatically fluent and topically coherent documents that subtly embed false or misleading information and rank competitively against authentic content. We instantiate FSAP in two modes: FSAP-IntraQ, which leverages harmful examples from the same query to enhance topic fidelity, and FSAP-InterQ, which enables broader generalization by transferring adversarial patterns across unrelated queries. Our experiments on the TREC 2020 and 2021 Health Misinformation Tracks, using four diverse neural ranking models, reveal that FSAP-generated documents consistently outrank credible, factually accurate documents. Furthermore, our analysis demonstrates that these adversarial outputs exhibit strong stance alignment and low detectability, posing a realistic and scalable threat to neural retrieval systems. FSAP also effectively generalizes across both proprietary and open-source LLMs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Few-Shot Adversarial Prompting (FSAP), a novel black-box attack framework that utilizes the in-context learning capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs) to generate high-ranking adversarial documents. Unlike previous methods that rely on token-level perturbations or manual rewriting, FSAP generates entirely new documents based on a small set of pre-existing harmful examples, bypassing the need for gradient access or model internals.  It comes in two flavors: FSAPIntraQ, which focuses on single-topic attacks using examples from the same query, and FSAPInterQ, which enables broader generalization by using examples from diverse queries. Experiments on TREC 2020/2021 Health Misinformation tracks, using various neural ranking models (NRMs), demonstrate that FSAP consistently generates documents that outrank credible sources while exhibiting high stance alignment and low detectability, posing a realistic threat to retrieval systems. The authors also show that FSAP generalizes across different LLMs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the framing of adversarial document generation as an in-context learning problem for LLMs. While previous work has explored LLM-generated misinformation, this paper specifically targets the vulnerability of *neural ranking models* through the injection of autonomously generated adversarial content *rather than manipulating existing documents*.  The distinction between FSAPIntraQ and FSAPInterQ, and the demonstration of generalization across queries are also noteworthy. The combination of autonomous generation, black-box access, and the focus on defeating NRMs is a valuable contribution. The evaluation of how well FSAP-generated examples are able to evade detection is also of significant importance.

**Significance:**  The significance of this work is considerable for several reasons:

*   **Addresses a realistic threat:** As LLMs become more prevalent, the potential for automated generation of misleading or harmful content increases. This paper directly addresses how such content can be used to manipulate search results.
*   **Practical attack strategy:** The black-box nature of FSAP, requiring only a few example prompts and access to an LLM's API, makes it a very practical and scalable attack vector. This is more realistic than approaches that require internal model access.
*   **Highlights a vulnerability in NRMs:**  The demonstration that these generated documents can consistently outrank credible sources reveals a weakness in current NRMs, particularly regarding their susceptibility to subtle yet persuasive content.
*   **Comprehensive evaluation:**  The experiments are well-designed, using established datasets and metrics suitable for evaluating adversarial attacks. The analysis of stance alignment and detectability adds depth to the evaluation, going beyond simple ranking performance.
*   **Generalizability across LLMs:** Shows that the developed method can be transferred between different LLM architectures, meaning the approach is not dependent on a specific LLM.

**Strengths:**

*   Clear problem statement and motivation.
*   Novel framework that effectively leverages LLMs for adversarial purposes.
*   Comprehensive evaluation using relevant datasets and metrics.
*   Thorough analysis of stance alignment, detectability, and few-shot learning's effect.
*   Open access of the code, prompts, and data used in the paper.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited dataset diversity:** While the TREC Health Misinformation tracks are valuable, they are specific to a single domain (health).  Evaluating FSAP on other domains, such as news or politics, would further strengthen the claim of generalizability.
*   **Reliance on GPT-4 for evaluation:** The assessment of stance alignment and detectability relies on GPT-4. While GPT-4 is a powerful LLM, biases in its classification are possible. An ablation study with another LLM would make the work more robust.
*   **Limited exploration of defense mechanisms:** The paper primarily focuses on the attack. While acknowledging the need for more robust NRMs, it doesn't delve into specific defense strategies in detail. Further research into how FSAP can inform the design of robust search systems would be a worthwhile extension.
*   There are no comparisons to more traditional information retrieval models in this paper, and these would be valuable given that modern neural retrieval models are being evaluated.

**Justification for the score:**

The paper introduces a novel and practical method for generating adversarial documents that can effectively deceive neural ranking models. The results demonstrate a significant vulnerability in current search systems. The methodology is sound and the evaluation is thorough. The weaknesses related to dataset diversity and reliance on a single evaluation LLM are minor and don't detract significantly from the core contribution. While a more detailed discussion of potential defenses would have been beneficial, the focus on the attack itself is well-justified given the novelty of the approach. Taking these points into account, the score given to the paper represents how impactful the approach is and how it shows a realistic vulnerability with neural ranking models.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.IR
- **Score**: 8/10

### Multiple Memory Systems for Enhancing the Long-term Memory of Agent
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15294v1)
- **Authors**: Gaoke Zhang, Bo Wang, Yunlong Ma, Dongming Zhao, Zifei Yu
- **Abstract**: An agent powered by large language models have achieved impressive results, but effectively handling the vast amounts of historical data generated during interactions remains a challenge. The current approach is to design a memory module for the agent to process these data. However, existing methods, such as MemoryBank and A-MEM, have poor quality of stored memory content, which affects recall performance and response quality. In order to better construct high-quality long-term memory content, we have designed a multiple memory system (MMS) inspired by cognitive psychology theory. The system processes short-term memory to multiple long-term memory fragments, and constructs retrieval memory units and contextual memory units based on these fragments, with a one-to-one correspondence between the two. During the retrieval phase, MMS will match the most relevant retrieval memory units based on the user's query. Then, the corresponding contextual memory units is obtained as the context for the response stage to enhance knowledge, thereby effectively utilizing historical data. Experiments on LoCoMo dataset compared our method with three others, proving its effectiveness. Ablation studies confirmed the rationality of our memory units. We also analyzed the robustness regarding the number of selected memory segments and the storage overhead, demonstrating its practical value.
- **Summary**: Okay, I've analyzed the provided research paper "Multiple Memory Systems for Enhancing the Long-term Memory of Agent". Here's a summary and a critical evaluation:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of effectively managing long-term memory in large language model (LLM)-powered agents.  It proposes a Multiple Memory System (MMS) inspired by cognitive psychology theories, specifically Tulving's memory system classification and the encoding specificity principle. MMS processes short-term memory into multiple long-term memory fragments (keywords, cognitive perspectives, episodic memory, and semantic memory), constructing retrieval memory units (for relevance matching) and contextual memory units (for knowledge enhancement during generation).  Experiments on the LoCoMo dataset demonstrate that MMS outperforms existing methods like Naive RAG, MemoryBank, and A-MEM in both retrieval and generation tasks.  Ablation studies validate the design of the memory units, and robustness analyses show practical value regarding the number of memory segments and storage overhead.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Inspired by Cognitive Psychology:** Grounding the approach in established cognitive psychology theories (Tulving's memory systems, levels of processing, encoding specificity) is a significant strength. This provides a theoretical foundation and differentiates it from purely engineering-driven solutions. It offers a more structured and interpretable approach to memory management.
    *   **Multi-Granularity Memory Fragments:** The decomposition of short-term memory into different types of fragments (keywords, cognitive perspectives, episodic memory, semantic memory) is a key contribution.  This multi-granularity approach allows for more nuanced retrieval and contextualization, leading to improved performance. Addressing the quality of stored memory is important and often overlooked.
    *   **Clear System Architecture:** The paper presents a well-defined and understandable architecture for MMS, including the construction of retrieval and contextual memory units. This clarity aids in reproducibility and potential extensions of the work.
    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** The experimental results on the LoCoMo dataset convincingly demonstrate the superiority of MMS over several baselines. The use of standard metrics (Recall@N, F1, BLEU-1) allows for objective comparison. The Ablation study helps rationalize the design of the components.
    *   **Robustness Analysis:** The evaluation of robustness concerning the number of memory segments and storage overhead is a positive aspect, showing consideration for practical deployment issues.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on LLMs:**  The system relies heavily on the capabilities of the underlying LLM for tasks such as extracting keywords, cognitive perspectives, and creating summaries. The quality of these operations directly influences the effectiveness of MMS. Further investigation is needed to determine its performance with lower capabilities of LLMs.
    *   **Dataset Limitations:** While LoCoMo is a suitable dataset for evaluating long-term memory, the system needs to be tested on a broader range of tasks and datasets to ensure generalization.
    *   **Limited Novelty in Individual Components:** While the integration of multiple memory types is novel, the individual memory types themselves (semantic, episodic) and techniques (RAG) have been explored previously in other works. The main novelty comes from the combination and specific configuration within the MMS framework.
    *   **Complexity:** The multi-stage process increases system complexity, adding computational overhead. A thorough computational complexity analysis should be incorporated.
    *   **Lack of Comparison on Different Sizes of Memory Storage:** It would be good to compare MMS performance against other long-term models to see how they measure up, based on storage overhead.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**
The paper provides a good combination of theoretical justification and practical implementation. Designing the memory units and matching them with the type of tasks helps achieve better performance on memory tasks.

*   **Potential Influence:**

    *   The MMS approach could inspire future research on more sophisticated memory architectures for LLM-powered agents.
    *   The idea of decomposing short-term memory into different fragment types could be adopted in other memory systems.
    *   The framework could be extended to incorporate other cognitive processes, such as attention mechanisms or reasoning modules.

**Justification for Score:**

Considering the strengths and weaknesses, a score of **7.5** seems appropriate. The paper makes a solid contribution by integrating cognitive psychology principles into a practical memory system for LLM agents. The multi-granularity approach and strong empirical results are notable strengths. The MMS system has the potential to influence the future direction of the research by combining theoretical justification with a good practical implementation. However, the reliance on LLMs, potential limitations in dataset generality, and a few complexities limit the assigned score.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### MLLMRec: Exploring the Potential of Multimodal Large Language Models in Recommender Systems
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15304v1)
- **Authors**: Yuzhuo Dang, Xin Zhang, Zhiqiang Pan, Yuxiao Duan, Wanyu Chen, Fei Cai, Honghui Chen
- **Abstract**: Multimodal recommendation typically combines the user behavioral data with the modal features of items to reveal user's preference, presenting superior performance compared to the conventional recommendations. However, existing methods still suffer from two key problems: (1) the initialization methods of user multimodal representations are either behavior-unperceived or noise-contaminated, and (2) the KNN-based item-item graph contains noisy edges with low similarities and lacks audience co-occurrence relationships. To address such issues, we propose MLLMRec, a novel MLLM-driven multimodal recommendation framework with two item-item graph refinement strategies. On the one hand, the item images are first converted into high-quality semantic descriptions using an MLLM, which are then fused with the textual metadata of items. Then, we construct a behavioral description list for each user and feed it into the MLLM to reason about the purified user preference containing interaction motivations. On the other hand, we design the threshold-controlled denoising and topology-aware enhancement strategies to refine the suboptimal item-item graph, thereby enhancing the item representation learning. Extensive experiments on three publicly available datasets demonstrate that MLLMRec achieves the state-of-the-art performance with an average improvement of 38.53% over the best baselines.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "MLLMRec: Exploring the Potential of Multimodal Large Language Models in Recommender Systems":

**Summary:**

The paper proposes MLLMRec, a novel multimodal recommendation framework that leverages Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) to improve recommendation accuracy. The core ideas include: 1) Using an MLLM to generate semantic descriptions of item images, fusing them with textual metadata to create enhanced item representations. 2) Constructing personalized user behavior descriptions from historical interactions, feeding these to an MLLM to reason about purified user preferences incorporating latent interaction motivations. 3) Employing threshold-controlled denoising and topology-aware enhancement strategies to refine the item-item graph, reducing noise and capturing audience co-occurrence.  The authors demonstrate through experiments on three datasets that MLLMRec outperforms state-of-the-art baselines.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

*   **Strengths:** The use of MLLMs for generating user preferences is a relatively new approach in recommender systems.  The explicit reasoning about user motivations, rather than relying solely on observed behaviors, is a valuable contribution. The item-item graph refinement strategies are also novel, addressing limitations of standard KNN-based graph construction. The combination of these three aspects leads to a new, potentially more informative approach to multi-modal recommender systems.

*   **Weaknesses:** While the individual components are well-defined, the core idea of utilizing LLMs for reasoning in recommendation is not entirely new.  Other studies have also explored LLMs for various aspects of recommendation (generating attributes, providing explanations, etc.). The item-item graph refinements are standard approaches from graph learning.  The novelty primarily lies in the specific combination and application within the context of multimodal recommendation. The performance gains are heavily dependent on the chosen MLLM model.

**Significance:**

*   **Strengths:**  The performance improvements demonstrated on the datasets are substantial, indicating a practical benefit of the proposed approach. The ablation studies provide insights into the importance of each component, strengthening the validity of the framework. The potential to reduce reliance on user-item graph convolution through more effective user preference generation is significant, as this part of recommendation systems can be computationally expensive. The work suggests a new direction for research in multi-modal recommendations by showing how MLLMs can be used to improve user and item representations and model interaction motivations.

*   **Weaknesses:** The experiments are limited to implicit feedback data from Amazon.  It's unclear how well the approach generalizes to other types of data or recommendation scenarios (e.g., explicit feedback, sequence-aware recommendation). The computational cost of using a large MLLM is a concern for real-world deployment. The framework's sensitivity to hyperparameter tuning requires careful consideration in practical applications. It might also be difficult to attribute performance improvements to a specific aspect of the reasoning, as the MLLM is a complex system with its own internal reasoning processes.

**Justification:**

While the core ideas have some overlap with existing research, the specific combination of MLLM-based preference reasoning with graph refinement strategies is novel, and the experimental results demonstrate clear improvements over baselines. The paper demonstrates a useful application of MLLMs in recommendation and gives us a potential alternative to graph-based user representation. There is also a clear demonstration that LLM usage can be beneficial to recommendation tasks and can result in improvements over current state of the art systems.

Score: 7

**Rationale:** The paper presents a solid contribution to the field with a clearly defined framework and experimental validation. The novelty is incremental but non-negligible, and the results indicate practical significance. It may inspire future research on integrating MLLMs into recommendation and refine current models. A higher score would require a more groundbreaking conceptual contribution or more extensive experimentation across various datasets and scenarios.

- **Classification**: cs.IR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Coarse-to-Fine Grounded Memory for LLM Agent Planning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15305v1)
- **Authors**: Wei Yang, Jinwei Xiao, Hongming Zhang, Qingyang Zhang, Yanna Wang, Bo Xu
- **Abstract**: Recent advancements in Large Language Models (LLMs) have driven growing interest in LLM-based agents for complex planning tasks. To avoid costly agent training, many studies adopted memory mechanism that enhances LLM with offline experiences or online trajectory analysis. However, existing works focus on single-granularity memory derived from dynamic environmental interactions, which are inherently constrained by the quality of the collected experiences. This limitation, in turn, constrain the diversity of knowledge and the flexibility of planning. We propose Coarse-to-Fine Grounded Memory (\Ours{}), a novel framework that grounds coarse-to-fine memories with LLM, thereby fully leverage them for flexible adaptation to diverse scenarios. \Ours{} grounds environmental information into coarse-grained focus points to guide experience collection in training tasks, followed by grounding of actionable hybrid-grained tips from each experience. At inference, \Ours{} retrieves task-relevant experiences and tips to support planning. When facing environmental anomalies, the LLM grounds the current situation into fine-grained key information, enabling flexible self-QA reflection and plan correction.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Coarse-to-Fine Grounded Memory (CFGM), a novel framework designed to enhance the planning capabilities of Large Language Model (LLM)-based agents in complex environments.  CFGM addresses the limitations of existing memory mechanisms that rely on single-granularity, dynamically-derived environmental interactions. It aims to improve both the quality of collected experiences and the flexibility of planning by grounding memories at multiple levels of granularity with the LLM's internal knowledge. The framework operates in three stages: 1) **Coarse-grained Focus-Driven Experience Collection:**  LLM-generated focus points guide the exploration process during training, 2) **Hybrid-grained Experience-Wise Tips Extraction:** Actionable tips with varying levels of detail are derived from collected experiences and stored in a dictionary and 3) **Fine-grained Trajectory Information Adaptive Planning:**  At inference, relevant experiences and tips are retrieved, and when anomalies occur, the LLM grounds the current situation into key information for flexible plan correction using self-QA. The authors demonstrate the effectiveness of CFGM through experiments on AlfWorld, WebShop, and ScienceWorld, showing improved performance and robustness compared to existing memory-enhanced agent systems.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The paper's core novelty lies in its multi-granularity memory approach that integrates LLM knowledge into the memory process itself rather than treating memory as a separate entity.  The combination of coarse-grained focus points to guide exploration, hybrid-grained tip extraction, and fine-grained anomaly handling is a novel synthesis of existing memory and reflection techniques. The specific method of using LLMs to generate the focus points and extract the tips appears to be the most novel aspect.

*   **Significance:** The paper tackles a significant problem: the limited adaptability and efficiency of LLM agents in complex environments. The approach addresses key weaknesses in current memory-augmented systems, improving both exploration efficiency and error correction. The results across multiple challenging benchmarks (AlfWorld, Webshop, ScienceWorld) suggest a significant performance improvement and a potential for broader impact. The ablation studies provide detailed insights into the contributions of each component, which strengthens the significance of the work. The out-of-distribution generalization experiments hint at improved knowledge transfer capabilities, adding another layer of importance.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper is well-written and clearly explains the CFGM framework.
    *   The experimental design is thorough, covering multiple environments, comparing against strong baselines, and performing extensive ablation studies.
    *   The results are compelling, showing statistically significant improvements over state-of-the-art memory-enhanced agent systems.
    *   The paper provides a detailed analysis of the different components, showcasing the benefits of each aspect of the coarse-to-fine memory approach.
    *   The inclusion of trajectory examples provides valuable insight into the operation of CFGM.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The reliance on GPT-4-Turbo may limit the accessibility and reproducibility of the research. While a study of generalization across models is presented, the performance using more accessible models could be more prominently featured.
    *   The paper could benefit from a more detailed discussion of the computational costs associated with CFGM, particularly the overhead of LLM-based focus point generation, tip extraction, and key information reflection.  A cost analysis would help readers assess the practical feasibility of the approach.
    *   The limitations section, while acknowledging certain constraints, could explore the scalability of CFGM to even more complex and dynamic environments, especially regarding the maintenance and update of the tips dictionary.

*   **Potential Influence:**  If the advantages in performance and adaptability prove to be consistent in more diverse and challenging settings, the CFGM approach could have a considerable impact on the design of LLM-based agents. The method of grounding memories at different granularities with the help of the LLM itself could become a standard approach. The emphasis on key information extraction and self-QA offers a useful strategy for mitigating reasoning errors. The study of out-of-distribution generalization highlights the importance of studying knowledge transfer in agent design.

*   **Rigorous Rationale**: Overall, the paper presents a well-researched and compelling approach to enhancing LLM-based agents. The multi-granularity memory grounding technique, along with the comprehensive experimentation and ablation studies, supports its practical value and potential significance in the field. While the limitations related to computational cost and scalability in extremely complex settings exist, the paper's merits outweigh its drawbacks.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale for the Score:** The paper makes a significant contribution by introducing a novel and effective method for enhancing LLM agent planning through multi-granularity memory grounding. The experimental results are strong, and the ablation studies provide valuable insights. While some concerns about computational cost and the reliance on closed-source models need to be addressed in future work, the overall quality and potential impact of the research warrant a score of 8. This indicates a strong contribution that is likely to influence future research in LLM-based agents and memory mechanisms.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 8/10

### VideoEraser: Concept Erasure in Text-to-Video Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15314v1)
- **Authors**: Naen Xu, Jinghuai Zhang, Changjiang Li, Zhi Chen, Chunyi Zhou, Qingming Li, Tianyu Du, Shouling Ji
- **Abstract**: The rapid growth of text-to-video (T2V) diffusion models has raised concerns about privacy, copyright, and safety due to their potential misuse in generating harmful or misleading content. These models are often trained on numerous datasets, including unauthorized personal identities, artistic creations, and harmful materials, which can lead to uncontrolled production and distribution of such content. To address this, we propose VideoEraser, a training-free framework that prevents T2V diffusion models from generating videos with undesirable concepts, even when explicitly prompted with those concepts. Designed as a plug-and-play module, VideoEraser can seamlessly integrate with representative T2V diffusion models via a two-stage process: Selective Prompt Embedding Adjustment (SPEA) and Adversarial-Resilient Noise Guidance (ARNG). We conduct extensive evaluations across four tasks, including object erasure, artistic style erasure, celebrity erasure, and explicit content erasure. Experimental results show that VideoEraser consistently outperforms prior methods regarding efficacy, integrity, fidelity, robustness, and generalizability. Notably, VideoEraser achieves state-of-the-art performance in suppressing undesirable content during T2V generation, reducing it by 46% on average across four tasks compared to baselines.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "VideoEraser: Concept Erasure in Text-to-Video Diffusion Models":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the growing concerns about the potential misuse of text-to-video (T2V) diffusion models for generating harmful or inappropriate content. It introduces VideoEraser, a training-free framework designed to prevent T2V diffusion models from generating videos with undesirable concepts, even when prompted explicitly to do so. VideoEraser operates as a plug-and-play module and integrates with existing T2V models through a two-stage process: (1) Selective Prompt Embedding Adjustment (SPEA), which adjusts the text embeddings of tokens likely to trigger the undesirable concept, and (2) Adversarial-Resilient Noise Guidance (ARNG), which steers latent noise away from the target concept during the diffusion process. The authors evaluate VideoEraser across various tasks, including object erasure, artistic style erasure, celebrity erasure, and explicit content erasure, demonstrating its effectiveness in suppressing undesirable content while preserving the integrity and fidelity of the generated videos.  The paper claims the approach is effective, robust against adversarial attacks, and generalizable across different T2V models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper demonstrates a reasonable degree of novelty. The idea of training-free concept erasure is not entirely new, as it has been explored in the text-to-image domain. However, adapting it to the T2V domain with the specific challenges posed by temporal coherence and the architecture of T2V models is a contribution. The two-stage approach with SPEA and ARNG is also novel in its design. Existing concept erasure methods for T2V diffusion models are limited, as stated in the paper. The evaluation framework that goes beyond just efficacy is another positive aspect of novelty.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a significant problem in the field of generative AI. As T2V models become more powerful and widely accessible, the ability to control the generated content and prevent misuse is critical. The practicality of the proposed method (training-free, plug-and-play) increases its potential for real-world adoption. Moreover, the paper introduces a comprehensive evaluation framework for T2V concept erasure.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Training-Free Approach:** Avoids the high computational costs and storage overhead associated with fine-tuning diffusion models.
    *   **Plug-and-Play Design:** Facilitates easy integration with mainstream T2V frameworks.
    *   **Two-Stage Process (SPEA + ARNG):** Addresses both token-level and latent noise aspects of concept erasure.
    *   **Robustness:** Designed to be resilient against adversarial attacks, which is a crucial consideration.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** Considers efficacy, integrity, fidelity, robustness, and generalizability.
    *   **Extensive Experiments:** Conducted on various tasks and models, providing substantial evidence for the method's effectiveness.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Computational Overhead:**  Although training-free, the method incurs some additional computational overhead during inference, potentially limiting its applicability in real-time scenarios. Although the authors state that "both the optimized vanilla classifier guidance and our method require the same time during inference," the fact that the initial (non-optimized) method requires 1.4x standard procedure is still a weakness
    *   **Abstract Concepts:** The paper acknowledges that VideoEraser is more effective for concrete concepts than for abstract ones. The lack of effectiveness against broader or abstract concepts (e.g., artistic style, nudity) is a limitation. As stated in the paper: "For more abstract concepts, VideoEraser may only partially eliminate targeted attributes such as stylistic elements."
    *   **Limited Baselines:** Though SAFREE is an important comparison point, there could be other potentially powerful baselines that focus specifically on noise-level guidance or attention manipulation (even if they're in the image generation domain)
    *   **Minor fidelity loss:** Although VideoEraser has relatively high fidelity, it can still have lower fidelity that simply outputting the source video

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the development of safer and more responsible T2V models. The VideoEraser framework and the proposed evaluation metrics can serve as valuable tools for researchers and practitioners in this field. The work can also inform the development of regulatory frameworks for generative AI.

*   **Justification for Score:** The paper presents a well-designed and thoroughly evaluated framework for concept erasure in T2V models. While not groundbreaking in its conceptual foundation, it addresses a practical and important problem with a novel and effective approach. The comprehensive evaluation and focus on robustness contribute significantly to the field. However, the limitations regarding computational overhead and abstract concepts, along with room for improvement in the experimental section, prevent it from achieving a higher score.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### RETAIL: Towards Real-world Travel Planning for Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15335v1)
- **Authors**: Bin Deng, Yizhe Feng, Zeming Liu, Qing Wei, Xiangrong Zhu, Shuai Chen, Yuanfang Guo, Yunhong Wang
- **Abstract**: Although large language models have enhanced automated travel planning abilities, current systems remain misaligned with real-world scenarios. First, they assume users provide explicit queries, while in reality requirements are often implicit. Second, existing solutions ignore diverse environmental factors and user preferences, limiting the feasibility of plans. Third, systems can only generate plans with basic POI arrangements, failing to provide all-in-one plans with rich details. To mitigate these challenges, we construct a novel dataset \textbf{RETAIL}, which supports decision-making for implicit queries while covering explicit queries, both with and without revision needs. It also enables environmental awareness to ensure plan feasibility under real-world scenarios, while incorporating detailed POI information for all-in-one travel plans. Furthermore, we propose a topic-guided multi-agent framework, termed TGMA. Our experiments reveal that even the strongest existing model achieves merely a 1.0% pass rate, indicating real-world travel planning remains extremely challenging. In contrast, TGMA demonstrates substantially improved performance 2.72%, offering promising directions for real-world travel planning.
- **Summary**: Here is a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces RETAIL, a novel dataset designed to address the limitations of existing travel planning systems that rely on explicit user queries, ignore environmental factors, and produce oversimplified plans.  RETAIL supports implicit queries, environmental awareness, and all-in-one travel plans with rich details.  The paper also proposes TGMA, a topic-guided multi-agent framework, to improve real-world travel planning abilities. Experimental results indicate that TGMA significantly outperforms existing models on RETAIL, demonstrating promise for real-world travel planning. The study focuses on creating a more practical and comprehensive system for travel planning through improved data and a more advanced model.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in the RETAIL dataset.  Existing datasets lack the complexity of real-world travel planning, especially in handling implicit user requests, environmental considerations, and detailed plan generation. The combination of a new dataset alongside a new framework increases the paper's novelty. However, the TGMA framework, while demonstrating improvements, builds upon existing multi-agent and topic-guided approaches.
*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a significant gap in the field by tackling the misalignment between automated travel planning and real-world scenarios. A system that understands implicit queries, adapts to environmental factors, and generates detailed, practical plans would be highly valuable. The high failure rate (low pass rate) of existing models highlights the difficulty of the task and emphasizes the potential impact of RETAIL and TGMA. While improving the state of data is vital in advancing more robust frameworks, the study could have demonstrated the benefit of the improved data using more frameworks or a simple framework.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Dataset Creation:** RETAIL appears to be a valuable contribution, filling a gap in the availability of comprehensive travel planning data. The construction process, including tourism knowledge base collection, data annotation, and quality control, seems thorough. The quality evaluation metrics used and the reported quality score demonstrate a commitment to data integrity.
    *   **Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the limitations of current travel planning systems and motivates the need for a more realistic approach. This problem definition is well-supported by examples and analysis.
    *   **Experimental Results:** The experimental evaluation, despite showing low baselines and modest improvements by TGMA, are promising given that the task's a difficult task.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Modest TGMA Improvements:** While TGMA shows improvements over baselines, the final pass rates are still relatively low (2.72% at most), indicating there is still significant room for improvement. Also, as other open-source models are included in the baselines, future work could use stronger baselines such as LLava or other multimodal frameworks.
    *   **Limited Framework Evaluation:** In the study, only RETAIL is used. Also, while the ablation study in the supplementary material provides some insights into the contributions of different components of TGMA, the framework would benefit from more extensive evaluation across a broader range of datasets and architectures.

*   **Potential Influence:** The RETAIL dataset could become a valuable resource for the travel planning research community, fostering further advancements in this area. TGMA provides a promising direction for future research, although significant improvements are needed to achieve truly practical performance. The work is likely to influence future research by focusing attention on the challenges of real-world travel planning, including implicit queries and environmental awareness.
* **Rigorous Critique:** While rigorous, there are several flaws in the process or presentation of information. The dataset construction has few checks/methods for biased information. The baselines are older frameworks and lack a direct comparison to multi-modal models with a multi-agent framework. It would have benefited the paper to at least test another recent and well-known framework.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a meaningful contribution by identifying critical limitations in existing travel planning systems and creating a dataset to address these limitations. Although the TGMA framework demonstrates moderate improvements but doesn't present a breakthrough in performance, the paper clearly showcases its challenges and has provided insights into the importance of decision-making and plan revisions.

Score: 6

The score reflects the significant contribution of the RETAIL dataset and the clear articulation of the problem. However, the limited performance gains of TGMA and the need for more rigorous evaluation across multiple datasets prevent a higher score.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 6/10

### DiagECG: An LLM-Driven Framework for Diagnostic Reasoning via Discretized ECG Tokenization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15338v1)
- **Authors**: Jinning Yang, Wen Shi
- **Abstract**: Electrocardiography plays a central role in cardiovascular diagnostics, yet existing automated approaches often struggle to generalize across clinical tasks and offer limited support for open-ended reasoning. We present DiagECG, a novel framework that integrates time-series and language modeling by enabling large language models to process 12-lead ECG signals for clinical text generation tasks. Our approach discretizes continuous ECG embeddings into symbolic tokens using a lead-independent encoder and quantization module. These tokens are then used to extend the vocabulary of LLM, allowing the model to handle both ECG and natural language inputs in a unified manner. To bridge the modality gap, we pretrain the model on an autoregressive ECG forecasting task, enabling the LLM to model temporal dynamics using its native language modeling capabilities. Finally, we perform instruction tuning on both ECG question answering and diagnostic report generation. Without modifying the core model, DiagECG achieves strong performance across tasks while maintaining generalization to out-of-distribution settings. Extensive experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of each component and highlight the potential of integrating symbolic ECG representations into LLMs for medical reasoning.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "DiagECG: An LLM-Driven Framework for Diagnostic Reasoning via Discretized ECG Tokenization" introduces a novel framework, DiagECG, that integrates electrocardiogram (ECG) time-series data with large language models (LLMs) for clinical text generation tasks like diagnostic report generation and question answering. DiagECG discretizes continuous ECG embeddings into symbolic tokens using a lead-independent encoder and quantization module.  These tokens are then used to extend the LLM's vocabulary, enabling the model to process both ECG signals and natural language in a unified manner.  The framework also includes autoregressive pretraining on an ECG forecasting task to help the LLM model temporal dynamics and instruction tuning for specific clinical tasks. The authors demonstrate that DiagECG achieves strong performance across tasks and datasets and maintains generalization in out-of-distribution settings.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The paper exhibits moderate novelty. The core idea of tokenizing time series data for LLM integration is not entirely new, as the paper acknowledges related work in the TS-as-text and TS-embedding-based approaches. However, DiagECG's specific contributions lie in:
