This is important as "overthinking" and redundant reasoning steps become more prevalent with larger models.
    *   **Weakness (TIR Implementation):** While the paper evaluates different TIR paradigms, the implementation of these paradigms themselves might not be entirely novel. PoT, MT-TIR, and TIT are established methods, although their systematic comparison across a diverse benchmark is valuable.

*   **Significance:**
    *   **Strength (Generalizability of TIR):** The paper provides strong empirical evidence for the generalizability of TIR, demonstrating that it's not just effective for mathematical tasks but also yields performance gains in other reasoning domains. This suggests that TIR is a fundamental technique for improving LLM reasoning across a broader range of applications.
    *   **Strength (Efficiency Gains):** The finding that TIR enhances reasoning efficiency, as measured by PAC and AUC-PCC, is significant.  It suggests that TIR helps LLMs focus their computational efforts on relevant aspects of the problem, reducing "overthinking" and leading to more streamlined reasoning paths.
    *   **Weakness (Scope of Evaluation):** While the paper explores different TIR paradigms, the depth of analysis for each paradigm could be improved. Certain paradigms, particularly within specialized models, lack a comprehensive analysis of both qualitative reasoning patterns and a deeper exploration of the computational complexity inherent in their respective workflows. A comparative breakdown, detailing the specific advantages and drawbacks across metrics, could enhance the overall impact of the research.
    *   **Weakness (Tool Selection):**  The paper doesn't fully address the impact of the specific tools used within the TIR framework. The effectiveness of TIR hinges on the selection of appropriate and high-quality tools. A more detailed discussion of the criteria for tool selection and the sensitivity of results to different tool choices would strengthen the analysis.

*   **Impact:**
    *   The REASONZOO benchmark will likely be adopted by other researchers in the field, providing a valuable resource for evaluating and comparing different LLMs and reasoning techniques.
    *   The PAC and AUC-PCC metrics could become standard metrics for assessing LLM efficiency, encouraging the development of models that are not only accurate but also computationally efficient.
    *   The empirical findings of the paper will help guide future research on TIR, focusing on strategies for improving tool integration and mitigating potential limitations.

*   **Limitations:**
    *   While token count serves as a good proxy for computational cost, it might not fully reflect the actual FLOPs or energy consumption of different LLMs.
    *   The specific tasks within REASONZOO may not be fully representative of all real-world reasoning scenarios.

**Score and Justification:**

I assign a score of **7.5** to this paper.

*Reasoning:*

The paper makes a valuable contribution by introducing a new benchmark (REASONZOO) that covers a broader range of reasoning domains than existing benchmarks. The proposed PAC and AUC-PCC metrics are novel and address an important aspect of LLM evaluation (reasoning efficiency). The empirical results are convincing and demonstrate the benefits of TIR.
However, there are some weaknesses. The implementation of TIR paradigms might lack complete novelty and the paper could benefit from a greater depth of analysis in certain evaluated paradigms, plus a deeper dive into the influence of tool selection.

Nevertheless, the paper's strengths outweigh its weaknesses. The new benchmark and metrics, combined with the compelling empirical evidence, make this a significant contribution to the field of LLM reasoning, paving the way for more holistic and cost-aware evaluations. The benchmark itself contributes to replicability and furthers evaluation of future LLMs. The score reflects the practical contributions and impact more than just the theoretical framework.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Language-Guided Tuning: Enhancing Numeric Optimization with Textual Feedback
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15757v1)
- **Authors**: Yuxing Lu, Yucheng Hu, Nan Sun, Xukai Zhao
- **Abstract**: Configuration optimization remains a critical bottleneck in machine learning, requiring coordinated tuning across model architecture, training strategy, feature engineering, and hyperparameters. Traditional approaches treat these dimensions independently and lack interpretability, while recent automated methods struggle with dynamic adaptability and semantic reasoning about optimization decisions. We introduce Language-Guided Tuning (LGT), a novel framework that employs multi-agent Large Language Models to intelligently optimize configurations through natural language reasoning. We apply textual gradients - qualitative feedback signals that complement numerical optimization by providing semantic understanding of training dynamics and configuration interdependencies. LGT coordinates three specialized agents: an Advisor that proposes configuration changes, an Evaluator that assesses progress, and an Optimizer that refines the decision-making process, creating a self-improving feedback loop. Through comprehensive evaluation on six diverse datasets, LGT demonstrates substantial improvements over traditional optimization methods, achieving performance gains while maintaining high interpretability.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Language-Guided Tuning: Enhancing Numeric Optimization with Textual Feedback":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Language-Guided Tuning (LGT), a novel framework that uses Large Language Models (LLMs) to intelligently optimize machine learning configurations. LGT uses "textual gradients," which are qualitative feedback signals, to complement numerical optimization. The system consists of three specialized LLM agents: an Advisor, an Evaluator, and an Optimizer. The Advisor proposes configuration changes, the Evaluator assesses progress, and the Optimizer refines the prompts used by the agents. LGT coordinates optimization across model architecture, training strategy, feature engineering, and hyperparameters, and the framework uses a self-improving feedback loop. The paper presents experimental results on six diverse datasets, showing substantial improvements over traditional optimization methods and demonstrating high interpretability.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Novelty:** The core idea of using LLMs to provide textual gradients to guide numerical optimization is novel and potentially powerful. The multi-agent framework is a structured approach to using LLMs in a coordinated way, addressing limitations of single-agent approaches.
    *   **Interpretability:** LGT provides high interpretability by offering natural language explanations for its optimization decisions. This contrasts with "black-box" optimization methods, which are less transparent. This transparency is crucial for trust and understanding.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper includes a solid evaluation on diverse datasets and tasks, demonstrating the versatility of the LGT framework. The performance gains are substantial in some cases, showing the potential of the approach.
    *   **Self-Improving Mechanism:** The self-improving feedback loop allows LGT to adapt and refine its prompts over time, which can improve its effectiveness.
    *   **Coordination of Configuration Dimensions:** LGT addresses the interdependence of configuration dimensions (model architecture, training, etc.), unlike methods that treat them as independent. This holistic approach is a significant advantage.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on LLMs:** The performance of LGT heavily depends on the capabilities of the underlying LLMs. If the LLMs are inaccurate or biased, the optimization process can be negatively affected. This dependency introduces a potential point of failure.
    *   **Computational Cost:** While the paper claims "adaptive complexity", the computational cost of using LLMs remains high, especially for large datasets and complex models. The time complexity analysis (O(T.(A costg + costtrain + costprompt))) is correct in that it updates only one configuration but using LLMs for *A costg* and *costprompt* is still significantly slower and potentially more expensive than alternative configuration update schemes (e.g. using gradient descent on meta parameters). The benefits need to outweigh this cost. While the paper shows strong results against typical baseline methods (e.g. Grid Search), it's unclear how this would compare against significantly more efficient neural network-based NAS methods.
    *   **Parameter Sensitivity and Prompt Engineering:** LLMs are sensitive to prompt design and parameter tuning. The paper may not fully address the potential challenges of designing effective prompts and tuning the LLMs for optimal performance. The reliance on "textual gradients" also presents the risk that these gradients, while interpretable to humans, may not always align perfectly with the true objective function, thus leading to suboptimal solutions.
    *   **Scalability:** While the paper includes some relatively large-scale datasets, scaling LGT to extremely large and complex models with numerous configuration options could be challenging due to the LLM inference costs and the increasing complexity of the optimization problem.
    *   **Theoretical Analysis:** The theoretical analysis provides some insights, but its practical implications are limited. The assumptions about the compactness of the configuration space and the Lipschitz continuity of the loss function may not always hold in real-world scenarios.

*   **Significance:**

    *   LGT offers a new perspective on configuration optimization by integrating natural language reasoning with numerical optimization techniques.
    *   It addresses the limitations of traditional "black-box" methods by providing interpretable explanations for optimization decisions.
    *   The multi-agent framework and the self-improving feedback loop are valuable contributions to the field of automated machine learning.
    *   The paper's ideas could inspire further research on using LLMs for other optimization tasks and for building more transparent and trustworthy machine learning systems.
    *   This work, while novel, will likely only be highly influential in cases where interpretability is paramount over pure performance gains or when compute resources are not a bottleneck.

**Overall Assessment:**

The paper presents a novel and interesting approach to configuration optimization. However, the reliance on LLMs, potential computational costs, and the need for careful parameter tuning and prompt engineering are important considerations. It would be useful to extend the comparisons to against SOTA NAS or AutoML methods for configuration optimization. The results do, however, show that LGT has the potential to significantly impact the field, especially in scenarios where interpretability is valued.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Discovering Hidden Algebraic Structures via Transformers with Rank-Aware Beam GRPO
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15766v1)
- **Authors**: Jaeha Lee, Gio Huh, Ning Su, Tony Yue YU
- **Abstract**: Recent efforts have extended the capabilities of transformers in logical reasoning and symbolic computations. In this work, we investigate their capacity for non-linear latent pattern discovery in the context of functional decomposition, focusing on the challenging algebraic task of multivariate polynomial decomposition. This problem, with widespread applications in science and engineering, is proved to be NP-hard, and demands both precision and insight. Our contributions are threefold: First, we develop a synthetic data generation pipeline providing fine-grained control over problem complexity. Second, we train transformer models via supervised learning and evaluate them across four key dimensions involving scaling behavior and generalizability. Third, we propose Beam Grouped Relative Policy Optimization (BGRPO), a rank-aware reinforcement learning method suitable for hard algebraic problems. Finetuning with BGRPO improves accuracy while reducing beam width by up to half, resulting in approximately 75% lower inference compute. Additionally, our model demonstrates competitive performance in polynomial simplification, outperforming Mathematica in various cases.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the ability of Transformers to discover hidden algebraic structures, specifically focusing on functional decomposition of multivariate polynomials.  It introduces a backward synthetic data generation pipeline to control problem complexity, trains transformer models via supervised learning, and proposes a novel reinforcement learning method called Beam Grouped Relative Policy Optimization (BGRPO) to improve beam search efficiency. Experiments demonstrate that BGRPO improves accuracy and reduces inference compute requirements.  The model also shows competitive performance in polynomial simplification compared to Mathematica.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** While Transformers have been applied to symbolic mathematics before, the specific problem of *non-linear latent pattern discovery* in functional decomposition is a relatively unexplored area. The backward data generation pipeline is a useful contribution, allowing for fine-grained control of problem difficulty. The proposed BGRPO method, which incorporates rank information from beam search into the reward function, is a novel and promising technique. The observation that beam search shows unusually large improvements on the task, compared to typical NLP applications, is also insightful.

*   **Significance:** Functional decomposition has applications in various scientific and engineering fields, making this a relevant problem.  The paper demonstrates that Transformers can tackle this NP-hard problem with some success, opening up possibilities for neural models to complement traditional symbolic computation methods. The improvement in beam search efficiency via BGRPO is practically significant, as it reduces the computational cost of inference.  The observation that the model learns generalizable principles rather than memorizing specific numerical relationships, as demonstrated by the distribution adaptation experiments, is particularly encouraging. The result of outperforming Mathematica is notable.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Well-defined problem and motivation.
    *   Systematic experimental evaluation across multiple dimensions (problem complexity, architecture scaling, distribution adaptation, search strategy).
    *   Novel BGRPO method with demonstrated improvements in beam search efficiency.
    *   Competitive performance against Mathematica in polynomial simplification.
    *   Thorough analysis of model behavior and error patterns.
    *   Clear presentation of results and insightful discussion.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The generalizability investigation is limited to univariate polynomials, relatively narrow coefficient ranges, and degrees. This constraint makes assessing the true scope of the discovered principles difficult, especially when comparing to Mathematica's capabilities.
    *   The reliance on beam search, even with BGRPO, still introduces a significant computational overhead during inference, limiting the scalability to truly complex expressions or high dimensionality. Further reducing reliance on beam search is desired for practical uses.
    *   While outperforming Mathematica on some problems is interesting, a deeper dive into the reasons *why* and specific limitations would strengthen the argument.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper's findings suggest that Transformers can learn and exploit hidden algebraic structures. The BGRPO method could be adapted to other symbolic computation tasks or problems with sparse solution spaces. The systematic evaluation methodology could serve as a template for future research in this area.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a significant contribution to the field by demonstrating the potential of transformers in tackling a challenging symbolic computation task (functional decomposition). The introduction of BGRPO is a practical innovation that improves the efficiency of beam search, and the competitive performance against Mathematica underscores the capabilities of the proposed approach.

The limitations regarding generalizability and computational overhead prevent a higher score. Scaling to more complex problems and moving away from beam search will be essential for broader adoption.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Visual Autoregressive Modeling for Instruction-Guided Image Editing
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15772v1)
- **Authors**: Qingyang Mao, Qi Cai, Yehao Li, Yingwei Pan, Mingyue Cheng, Ting Yao, Qi Liu, Tao Mei
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in diffusion models have brought remarkable visual fidelity to instruction-guided image editing. However, their global denoising process inherently entangles the edited region with the entire image context, leading to unintended spurious modifications and compromised adherence to editing instructions. In contrast, autoregressive models offer a distinct paradigm by formulating image synthesis as a sequential process over discrete visual tokens. Their causal and compositional mechanism naturally circumvents the adherence challenges of diffusion-based methods. In this paper, we present VAREdit, a visual autoregressive (VAR) framework that reframes image editing as a next-scale prediction problem. Conditioned on source image features and text instructions, VAREdit generates multi-scale target features to achieve precise edits. A core challenge in this paradigm is how to effectively condition the source image tokens. We observe that finest-scale source features cannot effectively guide the prediction of coarser target features. To bridge this gap, we introduce a Scale-Aligned Reference (SAR) module, which injects scale-matched conditioning information into the first self-attention layer. VAREdit demonstrates significant advancements in both editing adherence and efficiency. On standard benchmarks, it outperforms leading diffusion-based methods by 30\%+ higher GPT-Balance score. Moreover, it completes a $512\times512$ editing in 1.2 seconds, making it 2.2$\times$ faster than the similarly sized UltraEdit. The models are available at https://github.com/HiDream-ai/VAREdit.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces VAREdit, a novel framework for instruction-guided image editing based on visual autoregressive (VAR) modeling.  Unlike diffusion models which often lead to unintended edits due to their global denoising process, VAREdit reframes image editing as a next-scale prediction problem. This involves sequentially generating multi-scale target features conditioned on both the source image and text instructions. The key contribution lies in addressing the scale mismatch issue when using only finest-scale source features by introducing a Scale-Aligned Reference (SAR) module.  SAR injects scale-matched conditioning information in the first self-attention layer. Experiments show that VAREdit outperforms leading diffusion-based methods in both editing adherence (measured by GPT-Balance score) and efficiency, achieving comparable or better quality with significantly faster inference times.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in several aspects.  First, it's the first tuning-based VAR framework specifically designed for instruction-guided image editing. Second, the identification of the scale mismatch problem in the context of VAR-based image editing and the proposed SAR module are novel contributions. Prior work on VAR models for image generation didn't explicitly address the challenges arising in the editing scenario, especially when conditioning on source images. Although AR models for image editing exist, this is a novel application of VAR with a significant improvement to performance.
*   **Significance:** The significance of this work is multifaceted.
    *   *Improved Editing Adherence:* The core problem addressed (spurious edits and poor adherence to instructions) is a major limitation of existing diffusion-based methods.  VAREdit offers a compelling alternative that directly tackles this problem, as demonstrated by the substantial improvements in GPT-Balance score.
    *   *Enhanced Efficiency:* VAR models, in general, provide significant speed advantages over diffusion models. This is especially important for interactive image editing applications. The paper quantifies this advantage, showing that VAREdit achieves competitive or superior editing quality at a fraction of the computational cost.
    *   *New Research Direction:* The paper opens up a new research direction for VAR-based image editing. The analysis of scale dependencies and the introduction of the SAR module provide valuable insights that can guide future research in this area.  It also highlights the potential of AR models to overcome some of the limitations of diffusion models in the context of image editing.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   *Clear Problem Definition:* The paper clearly articulates the limitations of diffusion-based methods and motivates the use of VAR models for image editing.
    *   *Well-Designed Solution:* The SAR module is a simple yet effective solution to the scale mismatch problem. Its targeted application to the first self-attention layer demonstrates a deep understanding of the model's behavior.
    *   *Comprehensive Evaluation:* The paper provides a thorough evaluation using standard benchmarks and metrics, including human evaluation with GPT-4, demonstrating the practical benefits of the proposed approach.
    *   *Significant Performance Gains:*  The experimental results show substantial improvements over existing methods in both editing quality and efficiency.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   *Dependency on Pre-trained Model:* VAREdit relies on a pre-trained VAR model (Infinity). While this is a common practice, it means that the performance is limited by the capabilities of the underlying model. It would have been interesting to see how the framework performs with different pre-trained models.
    *   *Limited Exploration of SAR Variants:* The paper focuses on a specific implementation of the SAR module. Exploring alternative designs or adaptive strategies for scale-aligned conditioning could potentially lead to further improvements.
    *   *Generalizability:* While the paper demonstrates strong results on standard benchmarks, the generalizability of VAREdit to more complex or specialized editing tasks remains to be explored.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to significantly influence the field of instruction-guided image editing by promoting the use of VAR models and providing a practical framework for building high-quality, efficient editing systems. It also suggests that the benefits from attention scores analysis for model improvement can be effective.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to instruction-guided image editing. The proposed VAREdit framework addresses a key limitation of existing diffusion-based methods and offers a compelling alternative with improved editing adherence and efficiency.  The identification of the scale mismatch problem and the introduction of the SAR module are insightful and demonstrate a good understanding of the underlying models. The comprehensive evaluation provides strong evidence of the practical benefits of VAREdit. While the reliance on a pre-trained model and limited exploration of SAR variants are minor weaknesses, the overall impact of the paper is substantial.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### CineScale: Free Lunch in High-Resolution Cinematic Visual Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15774v1)
- **Authors**: Haonan Qiu, Ning Yu, Ziqi Huang, Paul Debevec, Ziwei Liu
- **Abstract**: Visual diffusion models achieve remarkable progress, yet they are typically trained at limited resolutions due to the lack of high-resolution data and constrained computation resources, hampering their ability to generate high-fidelity images or videos at higher resolutions. Recent efforts have explored tuning-free strategies to exhibit the untapped potential higher-resolution visual generation of pre-trained models. However, these methods are still prone to producing low-quality visual content with repetitive patterns. The key obstacle lies in the inevitable increase in high-frequency information when the model generates visual content exceeding its training resolution, leading to undesirable repetitive patterns deriving from the accumulated errors. In this work, we propose CineScale, a novel inference paradigm to enable higher-resolution visual generation. To tackle the various issues introduced by the two types of video generation architectures, we propose dedicated variants tailored to each. Unlike existing baseline methods that are confined to high-resolution T2I and T2V generation, CineScale broadens the scope by enabling high-resolution I2V and V2V synthesis, built atop state-of-the-art open-source video generation frameworks. Extensive experiments validate the superiority of our paradigm in extending the capabilities of higher-resolution visual generation for both image and video models. Remarkably, our approach enables 8k image generation without any fine-tuning, and achieves 4k video generation with only minimal LoRA fine-tuning. Generated video samples are available at our website: https://eyeline-labs.github.io/CineScale/.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the CineScale paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "CineScale: Free Lunch in High-Resolution Cinematic Visual Generation" addresses the challenge of generating high-resolution images and videos using diffusion models, which are typically trained on lower-resolution data. CineScale is an inference paradigm designed to enable existing pre-trained diffusion models (both UNet and DiT-based) to generate higher-resolution visual content without retraining or extensive fine-tuning.  It proposes techniques such as tailored self-cascade upscaling, restrained dilated convolution, and scale fusion to address issues like repetitive patterns and blurriness that arise during high-resolution generation. The paper presents both quantitative and qualitative results demonstrating the effectiveness of CineScale for text-to-image (T2I), text-to-video (T2V), image-to-video (I2V), and video-to-video (V2V) tasks, achieving 8k image generation and 4k video generation with minimal LoRA fine-tuning.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of CineScale, using a combination of targeted upscaling, modified convolutions, and scale fusion, offers a practical approach to leverage pre-trained models for higher resolution outputs. While some components build upon existing techniques (e.g., dilated convolutions from ScaleCrafter, self-cascade upscaling), the specific combination and adaptation for both UNet and DiT architectures, along with the minimal LoRA tuning strategy, represent a novel contribution. A key novelty lies in its application to both image and *video* generation across multiple tasks (T2I, T2V, I2V, V2V), extending the scope beyond typical image-centric approaches.  The NTK-RoPE adaptation for DiT models is also a valuable contribution.

*   **Significance:** The significance lies in the practicality and accessibility it offers. Eliminating the need for extensive retraining or large high-resolution datasets makes high-resolution generation more accessible to researchers and practitioners with limited resources. Achieving 8k images without any fine-tuning is a notable accomplishment.  The application to video tasks and the achieved 4k video generation with minimal LoRA fine-tuning is potentially impactful given the growing demand for high-quality video content. The comprehensive evaluation across different tasks and model architectures provides strong evidence for its effectiveness.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Comprehensive evaluation across multiple tasks (T2I, T2V, I2V, V2V).
    *   Compatibility with both UNet and DiT-based architectures.
    *   Significant results in high-resolution generation (8k images, 4k videos) without extensive training.
    *   Tuning-free for image generation.
    *   Minimal LoRA tuning for video generation.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Despite minimal LoRA fine-tuning for the video part, it is still required for high res video generation, and it would be even better if this tuning step could be further simplified or eliminated altogether.
    *   While the method is described as "free lunch," the computational cost of inference at such high resolutions is still significant, especially for video.
    *   While it addresses certain artifacts, it does so by building upon multiple existing works which can add complexity.
    *   The quantitative metrics, especially those involving resizing for comparison, may not fully capture the perceptual quality improvements.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by providing a practical and accessible solution for high-resolution generation. It also encourages further research into improving inference efficiency and developing more robust methods that require even less fine-tuning. The emphasis on video generation and its various tasks will likely spur more activity in that area.

*   **Critical Point:** The paper does not address any ethical considerations of high-resolution imagery and video generation, which is expected nowadays in CVPR/ICCV papers.

Overall, CineScale presents a valuable contribution by making high-resolution visual generation more practical and accessible. While there are areas for improvement (inference efficiency, potentially further minimizing or removing LoRA tuning), the strengths outweigh the weaknesses. The paper provides a well-evaluated and promising approach with the potential to significantly impact the field.

**Score: 7.5**

*   *Justification:* The score reflects a strong and practical solution to a current issue in generative AI. The 7.5 acknowledges the practical strengths, the adaptation across multiple architectures and tasks, and the promise of the results. It is, however, not a 9 or 10 because the novelty relies on a combination of existing ideas, the limited ethical consideration, and LoRA fine-tuning in video tasks means that the "free lunch" claim is a bit overstated. Also, while effective, the method doesn't introduce a radically new theoretical understanding.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10
